# Kubernetes deployment tree (generated planning inventory)

> PROPOSED, not deployed. Do not edit this file. Regenerate with the command in [Kubernetes deployment contract](../../../docs/kubernetes-deployment-contract.md#regeneration-and-review).

Inputs: [registry](../registry.yaml), [model](k8s-model.json); grammar version 2; run `review`; revision `r1`.

Registry: 7 standalone + 3 products + 1 suite = 11 tier scopes. No unregistered fourth product is synthesized.

Selected inventory: 55 releases + 1 shared infrastructure namespace = 56 namespaces; 147 backend pools; sum(pool.min) = 294 app pods; 63 logical databases on EACH PostgreSQL node; 84 active SQLite claims.

Default full-matrix derivation: (7 standalone members + (1 + 5 + 1) product members + 7 suite members) = 21 member occurrences per mode. Pools per member across pg/mixed-mem/mixed-disk/mem/disk = 1+2+2+1+1. At min=max=2: `21*7*2 = 294` app pods; `21*3 = 63` PostgreSQL databases; `21*2*2 = 84` SQLite claims. These baseline formulas do not override selected inputs.

A pool is a replica group, NOT another product. In mixed modes a PS-ID has two pools (four default pods). SQLite pods have independent databases, not HA/shared state. `i01` maps to ordinal `0`, `i02` to `1`; later ordinals are new slots.

Pod names are StatefulSet controller identities. PVC names follow `data-<statefulset>-<ordinal>`. Listed dormant max-capacity pods/PVCs are reservations, not claims that controllers have created them. All Secrets below are names only, never material.

Local cluster grammar: Compose project `cu-review-cluster`, Docker network `cu-review-nodes`, K3s server `cu-review-server-0`, workers `cu-review-agent-{0,1}`. Bootstrap is planned, not generated here. Host ports and node counts will be configurable.

## Shared infrastructure: `cu-review-infra`

Owner: cluster bootstrap/infra Helm release, never an application release. Namespace selectors admit only explicit run+release clients. Database roles deny other releases' databases. Shared LGTM is run-level observability, NOT mutually untrusted tenant isolation.

Steady-state dependency pods: collector=2, LGTM=1; when PostgreSQL is selected, leader=1 and logical follower=1 (not a failover pair). Only the actual selected dependencies are listed below. Transient Jobs and cluster system pods are excluded.

## `p:identity:mixed-mem`

Namespace `cu-review-p-identity-mixed-mem`; Helm release `cu-p-identity-mixed-mem`; labels `cu.io/run=review`, `cu.io/release=cu-p-identity-mixed-mem`. Namespace is bootstrap-owned; the app Helm release owns its non-retained resources.

## `u:cryptoutil:disk`

Namespace `cu-review-u-cryptoutil-disk`; Helm release `cu-u-cryptoutil-disk`; labels `cu.io/run=review`, `cu.io/release=cu-u-cryptoutil-disk`. Namespace is bootstrap-owned; the app Helm release owns its non-retained resources.

| `sm-kms-disk-1` | `sm-kms-disk-i02` | `sm-kms-disk-1-tls` | `data-sm-kms-disk-1` | `sm-kms-sqlite-2` | active |

Exact pod DNS / local TLS-SNI names (no host port per app):

- `sm-kms-disk-0.sm-kms-disk-pods.cu-review-u-cryptoutil-disk.svc.cluster.local` -> `sm-kms-disk-i01.cu-review-u-cryptoutil-disk.localhost`; instance Service `sm-kms-disk-i01.cu-review-u-cryptoutil-disk.svc.cluster.local:8080`.
- `sm-kms-disk-1.sm-kms-disk-pods.cu-review-u-cryptoutil-disk.svc.cluster.local` -> `sm-kms-disk-i02.cu-review-u-cryptoutil-disk.localhost`; instance Service `sm-kms-disk-i02.cu-review-u-cryptoutil-disk.svc.cluster.local:8080`.

## Isolation and validation boundary

- Shared infrastructure is an intentional exception to namespace/network isolation; allowlists, SQL grants, per-pod client certificates, and telemetry resource attributes preserve provenance.
- Release Secrets/RBAC, network rules, resource requests, trust bundles, storage class, image digests, and probe parameters must be validated against rendered Helm objects in implementation. This inventory names their owners; it is NOT a Helm renderer or live-cluster verifier.
- Actual Pod/PVC inventory uses active/min versus reserved/max semantics. Controller-created resource UIDs/IPs and Job pod suffixes are runtime-assigned and must be matched by ownerReferences/labels, never predicted.
